Google, Meta, TikTok sue over social media law

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Three of the biggest social media companies want to block a California law designed to reduce youth social media addiction from going into law.

On Wednesday, Google, Meta, and TikTok each filed lawsuits against California's attorney general, Rob Bonta.

Beginning on Jan. 1, 2027, the bill (AB 1709) would prohibit platforms including Google, Meta, and TikTok from providing "an addictive feature" to users under 16. The law describes such features as "psychologically exploitative" and intended to maximize engagement in a way that leads to compulsive use. This includes infinite scroll, autoplay, and an "addictive" feed.

The law also requires platforms to "implement reasonable measures" so that young users aren't offered such features.

The companies argue that the law infringes on their free speech rights, as well as Section 230, a provision of a 1996 federal law designed to shield online platforms from liability related to user content. Critics of Section 230 say it wrongly provides online platforms with broad immunity.

Google claimed in its lawsuit that its subsidiary, YouTube, personalizes recommendations to ensure that young users receive content relevant to their interests and appropriate for their age and developmental needs. The company defended autoplay as helping users enjoy content uninterrupted and sequentially, like a playlist for educational videos.

"AB 1709's bans on personalized recommendations and autoplay would therefore frustrate YouTube's efforts to provide under-16 users with safe, responsible, and enriching experiences on the platform," the lawsuit said. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile Products available for purchase through affiliate links. If you buy something through links on our site, Mashable may earn an affiliate commission.

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

Meta said that its services "collectively enable billions of people around the world to share ideas, offer support, discuss important topics." Prohibiting personalized feeds and autoplay, would "make it unlawful for Meta to disseminate speech to minors altogether," the company argued.

Lennon Torres, director of movement partnerships at the advocacy organization Heat Initiative, rejected the companies' arguments. Torres, a former child influencer, worked with the bill's author to address First Amendment concerns.

"They're framing this as a free speech fight, but what's really at stake is a business model that profits from keeping kids scrolling, even when it puts them at risk," Torres said. (Torres is a Mashable contributor.)

California's Attorney General's office told Reuters that it was reviewing the filings.

"Our office is committed to defending California's commonsense laws to protect kids online," the office said in a statement to Reuters.

In August, Meta settled a youth social media safety and addiction case for $17 billion, though the company did not admit guilt or liability. As part of that settlement, Meta agreed to limit teens' daily time spent on Instagram and Facebook to two hours; block access to both platforms overnight and during school hours; give teens the option to create a default non-algorithmic feed; and allow teens to turn off autoplay.

Meta also issued a "call to action" to YouTube and TikTok to adopt what the company describes as a new "industry standard." Neither company joined the settlement terms.

The New York Times Company is in talks”exclusive negotations” to acquire Letterboxd, the social network for film lovers, for around $330 million, The New York Times reports. The Times Company, Sony Pictures Entertainment, and A24 were all recently rumored to be among those interested in buying Letterboxd.

The negotiations with the Times Company have included the potential for Letterboxd’s co-founders to stay in charge but report to a Times Company executive. Letterboxd would likely remain standalone for the “foreseeable future,” according to the NYT. The company would also integrate the Times Company’s ad tech.

NYT spokesperson Charlie Stadtlander declined to comment. Letterboxd and current parent company Tiny Ltd. didn’t immediately reply to a request for comment from The Verge.

If the NYT does acquire Letterboxd, it would follow other major purchases like Wordle (in a deal in “the low seven figures”), Wirecutter (“slightly more than $30 million”), and The Athletic ($550 million).

Palantir Is Down 16% From Its High. History Suggests This Is What Happens Next.

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Palantir stock is selling off despite another round of strong quarterly results. Where does the stock go from here?

Palantir Technologies' (PLTR -9.73%) strong business results have supported significant valuation gains for the stock. The third quarter saw the company's revenue increase 96% year over year to $2.31 billion, and its net income surged 137% from the prior-year period to roughly $1.13 billion.

Despite the strong overall results, the company's stock has pulled back in subsequent trading due to concerns about slowing growth in commercial contract values, slower expansion in international markets, and bearish pressure on companies in the broader artificial intelligence (AI) space.

Palantir stock is down roughly 1% year to date, and the company's share price is down 16% from the lifetime high it reached last month. What does history suggest happens next for the stock?

In its recent quarterly report, Palantir guided for sales between $2.649 billion and $2.653 billion in the current quarter. Meanwhile, non-GAAP (generally accepted accounting principles) adjusted operating income is projected to be between $1.64 billion and $1.644 billion.

The company has built a very strong track record of surpassing its own guidance and Wall Street estimates, and this outperformance has generally helped generate wins for investors who bought Palantir stock and took a buy-and-hold approach.

Of course, investors have to keep in mind that past performance does not dictate future results. While Palantir has been a publicly traded company for a little over six years, that's still a relatively short period in the grand scheme of things.

The company has gone through periods when sentiment toward tech stocks turned negative. Still, its overall history as a publicly traded company has been characterized by a sector valuation backdrop that has generally been very favorable. For context, the S&P 500 is up roughly 125% since Palantir went public, and the even more tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite is up 127% over the same period.

Palantir looks poised to continue delivering results that could support substantial valuation gains. The company is growing sales at a highly impressive clip, and its asset-light software business is serving up margins that are nothing short of incredible. Thus far, the incredible margin strength has also shown a high degree of sustainability.

On the other hand, it's important to understand that Palantir will not be immune to broader market pressures, even if overall business performance remains quite robust. Investors who follow the market actively are almost certainly aware of concerns that valuations for AI stocks are in a bubble, as well as a slew of macroeconomic risk factors, including relatively high inflation, elevated bond yields, and other dynamics that threaten bullish momentum.

Trading at roughly 109 times this year's expected earnings and 51 times expected sales, Palantir's heavily growth-dependent valuation profile comes with substantial risk. However, the company is a clear category leader in vital corners of the AI software market — and there's a good chance that long-term investors will score wins with the stock even if near-term market pressures roil the market.

Keith Noonan has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Palantir Technologies. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

It’s That Time Of Year When More Wild Will Ferrell Elf Stories Are Coming Down The Pipeline

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

An Alan Ritchson And Arnold Schwarzenegger Buddy Flick Is The Christmas Movie I Didn't Know I Needed

I Didn't Expect Sydney Sweeney To Channel Kevin McCallister And Home Alone For Latest Lingerie Ad

Upcoming Hallmark Movies: Full List Of New Premiere Dates, Including The 2026 Countdown To Christmas

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The holiday season has officially begun, and while there are dozens of upcoming Christmas movies to watch, it’s always nice to revisit some of the best Christmas movies ever made, like Elf. Despite the Will Ferrell flick being over 20 years old, there are still so many stories coming out that have never been told before. Now, even more stories are coming out thanks to a new behind-the-scenes book co-written by Elf costar Andy Richter, titled Elf Unwrapped: Behind the Scenes of a Modern Christmas Classic.

Back in 2020, the classic Christmas film was featured on an episode of the Netflix docuseries The Christmas Movies That Made Us. Fans learned a lot of behind-the-scenes facts about the Jon Favreau-directed flick, including the dark ending the film almost received. Elf super fans already know that a large chunk of the interior scenes of the film were shot at an abandoned mental institution. Part of this was to keep costs down since the film was made on a $33 million budget. Richter talked about it with Us Weekly:

There was this old mental hospital that had a couple of empty wings, and that’s where the offices are, the Hobbs’ home, the mailroom; almost all the interiors of the movie are in a mental hospital that was still functioning a little bit. There was still a wing of the hospital that had patients in it.

Money wasn’t the only reason for the unusual filming location, according Ritcher told the outlet. The studio and soundstage space was limited due to a“big vampire movie” that was filming at the same time, so the production design team behind Elf made the unusual filming location work. It turned the space into the Gimbels department store, Greenway Press, and the Hobbs’ apartment. As magical as the locations looked on screen, some actors still found filming at the semi-functioning mental hospital unsettling, including Richter, as he said in the interview:

I wasn’t wandering around.. I went to the set. I went to the trailer. I got in the van. I went back to the hotel.

While filming aimed to stay on the abandoned side of the hospital, portions of the location were still occupied by patients. It’s unclear if any patients actually saw Ferrell traipsing around in his iconic green costume inspired by Rankin/Bass’s elves in Rudolph the Red-Nosed Reindeer, but Ritcher did speculate about what they might have thought if they caught a glimpse of the SNL alum:

Will was all over that place for a month or two, like what people were thinking? Patients were looking out the window and seeing this guy in February wandering around in an elf costume, and maybe recognizing, ‘Hey, isn’t that Will Ferrell?’

Ferrell himself had some apprehensions about the costume the first time he tried it on, so it’s valid for Richter to consider what outsiders who had no idea a Christmas movie was being filmed next door might have thought. Thankfully, it all worked out, and Elf continues to be a holiday movie must-watch for families.

Elf Unwrapped: Behind the Scenes of a Modern Christmas Classic is available at bookstores now, but if you want a more instant Elf fix, you can stream the film with an HBO Max subscription. And who knows, maybe Ferrell will dress up as Buddy the Elf once again to help promote the book.

Your Discord bot's timestamp says the year 58970 (I tested 16 edge cases)

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 5 • Originally published at discordtimestampgen.com • Fully Autonomous

Discord turns into a date shown in each reader's own time zone. The format is documented, but what Discord does with bad input isn't. Bots generate these strings, and bots get the input wrong, so on 4 November 2026 I sent 16 malformed or extreme codes to a private server from the Discord web client and read back what rendered.

(Times are as shown to a reader in UTC+5:30. Raw data: results.json.)

Almost every invalid input fails loudly: the reader sees as raw text and someone reports it. Milliseconds don't fail. Date.now() in JavaScript and System.currentTimeMillis() in Java return milliseconds. Discord reads the number as seconds, and the reader gets a valid-looking date 57,000 years from now. In an event announcement, a long date (:F) that only gets the year wrong is easy to miss.

Python's time.time() and datetime.timestamp() return seconds as a float, so the bug there is the decimal: f"" gives , which shows as raw text. Wrap it in int().

The upper bound isn't 2038 and it isn't 64 bits. 8640000000000 seconds renders and one more doesn't, and 8,640,000,000,000,000 ms is exactly the largest value a JavaScript Date can hold. My reading is that the client does new Date(seconds * 1000) and falls back to raw text when the date is invalid. That's an inference from the boundary, not something Discord documents.

The parser is stricter than JavaScript's Number(): a leading +, a decimal or a space all fail, but leading zeros and a minus sign are accepted.

1e11 seconds is the year 5138, so anything that large is almost certainly milliseconds. The same heuristic is used in the converter on discordtimestampgen.com, which also has a JSON API that returns all seven formats.

Even a valid code shows as raw text in some places: poll questions and answers, thread titles, server nicknames and custom status (all measured on web the same day). It does render in messages, thread messages and channel topics. I didn't test bot embeds or the mobile apps, which may differ.

Why Celery Workers Spawn Zombie Tasks on Hard Termination: The Mechanics of Timeout Propagation

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Graceful shutdowns in distributed task execution engines like Celery are notoriously deceptive. On paper, orchestration platforms like Kubernetes dispatch a SIGTERM to the container, the event loop captures the POSIX signal, triggers a cancellation cascade, runs resource cleanup routines, and exits cleanly with status code 0.

In real-world production environments, however, tasks frequently enter an unrecoverable "zombie" state. A worker is terminated, the broker assumes the task is either lost or stuck, and downstream data stores are left holding uncommitted transactions, open socket descriptors, or deadlocks.

This deep dive analyzes the internal mechanics of worker termination, evaluates a complete async shutdown simulation using modern Python tooling (asyncio, Pydantic v2, and orjson), breaks down the failure modes that sabotaged this implementation in testing, and provides the production-grade architectural patterns required to guarantee fault isolation.

The simulation architecture was designed to reproduce Celery worker process termination within an asynchronous event loop. The design intended to enforce strict type validation on task payloads via Pydantic v2 and ultra-low-latency state dumping via orjson.

The intended execution lifecycle proceeds as follows:

Below is the Python implementation designed to model the worker lifecycle, signal capture, task cancellation, and resource reclamation:

💡 For immediate deployment: The complete source code suite (ZIP) for this architecture is available on Gumroad for $0+ (Pay What You Want).

During test execution across automated build verification stages and containerized test runners, this implementation failed to transition past initialization. Three distinct structural vulnerabilities caused catastrophic failures:

The script contained docstrings hinting at installation ($ pip install orjson pydantic), but the application runtime initialization was fully decoupled from the environment provisioning layer.

When invoked inside a vanilla scratch image or an unprovisioned test runner, module loading exploded at parse-time before a single instruction in main() could execute:

Because third-party binary C-extensions (orjson) and complex validation frameworks (pydantic) were imported at the module root, the failure mode was unconditional. Any downstream testing tooling relying on simple introspections or CLI dry-runs died immediately.

A well-intentioned decision was made to omit fallback implementations (e.g., standard json and dataclasses) in the name of strict coding standards and zero-cost performance optimizations.

While valid for monolithic containerized production pods, this created an anti-pattern for lightweight verification routines and CLI tools. Stripping runtime degradation mechanisms eliminated operational portability. In air-gapped test subnets, bootstrapping containers, or edge runners lacking pre-compiled C-extension wheels, the script became completely unusable.

The code attempted platform segregation inside main():

However, this guard was fundamentally flawed:

Diagnosing the failure modes of this Celery graceful shutdown prototype reveals critical guidelines for building resilient distributed worker services:

Operational runbooks stating "run pip install" fail in automated pipelines. Dependency trees must be strictly enforced via declarative configurations (pyproject.toml, lockfiles, or multi-stage Docker builds). For standalone worker testing scripts, implement graceful dynamic imports that degrade to Python's standard library (json, dataclasses, and urllib) when specialized performance libraries are unavailable.

Signal handling interacts directly with the host kernel. When designing code that must execute in heterogeneous environments (e.g., developers on Windows/macOS vs. Linux containers in Kubernetes), direct calls to loop.add_signal_handler() must be encapsulated behind an abstraction adapter or unified signal dispatcher pattern.

Premature optimization that couples code directly to high-throughput C-extensions (orjson) at the expense of environment portability introduces unneeded brittleness. High-throughput data pipelines require orjson, but bootstrapping routines, monitoring hooks, and shutdown orchestrators should always possess self-healing fallback paths to guarantee system observability under hostile runtime conditions.

If this engineering log saved your production server (and your sanity), consider supporting our architecture on GitHub Sponsors.

Codex MCP: add, configure and debug MCP servers in Codex CLI

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 5 • Originally published at vibeide.dev

Codex can call tools from Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers: documentation lookups, browsers, design files, issue trackers and more. You add a server once, and the Codex CLI, the Codex app and the IDE extension all pick it up because they share one configuration. This guide shows the exact codex mcp commands and config.toml keys from the official Codex MCP documentation and the open source Codex CLI, checked in November 2026.

Quick answer: run codex mcp add -- for a local (stdio) server or codex mcp add --url for a remote (Streamable HTTP) server, then start Codex and type /mcp to see the connected servers. Every server is stored as an [mcp_servers.] table in ~/.codex/config.toml, or in .codex/config.toml for one trusted project.

MCP is an open protocol that lets Codex use tools and context from outside programs. Codex supports two kinds of servers:

Both kinds end up in the same list, and Codex can call their tools while it works on your task.

Use codex mcp add. The usage line in the Codex source is codex mcp add [OPTIONS] (--url | -- ...): give the server a name, then either a command after -- or a URL, never both.

A local stdio server, here the Context7 documentation server used as the example in the Codex docs:

Pass environment variables to a stdio server with --env KEY=VALUE. You can repeat the flag, and it only works for stdio servers:

A remote Streamable HTTP server, with the token read from an environment variable instead of stored in the file:

Then start Codex in your project and type /mcp to check that the server connected and see what it offers.

These are the codex mcp subcommands as defined in the Codex CLI source in November 2026:

codex mcp list --json is handy in scripts, for example to check in CI that a required server is configured before you run codex exec. Our codex exec guide covers non-interactive runs.

Every server lives in a [mcp_servers.] table. You can edit the file directly instead of using codex mcp add. The stdio example from the docs:

env sets variables for the server process, and env_vars forwards variables that already exist in your shell by name. The Streamable HTTP example:

The keys you will use most, as listed in the docs in November 2026:

Keep secrets out of the file where you can. bearer_token_env_var, env_vars and env_http_headers all read values from your environment, so the config can be committed without the token.

Codex reads MCP servers from two places. ~/.codex/config.toml is the default and applies to every project. A .codex/config.toml file inside a repository adds servers for that project only, and the docs state that it works only in trusted projects.

A good split: personal tools such as a documentation server go in the global file, and tools the whole team needs for one repository (a browser for end-to-end tests, the project's issue tracker) go in the project file, committed next to your AGENTS.md. Our guide to AGENTS.md for Codex covers the instruction side of the same setup.

Many remote servers use OAuth instead of a static token. Run codex mcp login and finish the sign-in in your browser. On a machine without a browser, --no-browser prints the authorization URL and lets you paste the callback URL back. --scopes read,write overrides the scopes for one login.

If the server needs a pre-registered OAuth client, add it with the client id:

The docs say the default callback is http://127.0.0.1/callback, and that the top-level mcp_oauth_callback_port and mcp_oauth_callback_url settings fix the port or URL when a server only accepts a specific redirect. codex mcp logout removes the stored credentials.

A server can expose far more tools than you want an agent to call. Two keys narrow the list: enabled_tools is an allow list, and disabled_tools is a deny list that the docs say is applied after enabled_tools.

For approvals, the docs list default_tools_approval_mode per server (values auto, prompt, writes or approve) and a per-tool override under tools..approval_mode. tools..output_token_limit caps how much one tool's output can add to the context. MCP tools sit next to Codex's own sandbox and approval settings, which our Codex sandbox guide explains.

A useful first check for any stdio server is to run its command and args by hand in the same shell. If it fails there, it will fail inside Codex too.

Older guides show Codex started as an MCP server so another agent can call it, with tools named codex and codex-reply. OpenAI's own cookbook now marks that Codex MCP server as deprecated and kept for existing integrations, so for new automation look at codex exec or the Codex SDK instead.

If you also use Claude Code, you will configure many of the same servers there. Our Claude Code MCP guide covers its claude mcp add commands and scopes, and Codex vs Claude Code compares the two CLIs. VibeiDE is a desktop app that coordinates the Codex, Claude Code and OpenCode CLIs you already installed, so each CLI keeps its own MCP configuration; Codex runs in an embedded terminal with its original interface. Each project has its own task queue, and finished tasks wait in Ready for review. See the Codex page or start a 14-day trial, without a card, from the download page.

Moonshot Just Put a 2.8-Trillion-Parameter Model on Hugging Face — Kimi K3.1 Pro's Open Weights Are Real, and the Fine Print Matters

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 5 • Originally published at convertelementor.com

Originally published on ConvertElementor: https://convertelementor.com/blog/moonshot-just-put-a-2-8-trillion-parameter-model-on-hugging-face-kimi-k3-1-pro-s-open-weights-are-real-and-the-fine-print-matters-november-2026-20261105

Moonshot AI published the full weights of Kimi K3.1 Pro on Hugging Face on November 4, 2026 — 2.8 trillion parameters, a 1-million-token context window, and a license that is genuinely open for most of us but not for everyone.

Moonshot posted the weights on Hugging Face under the repository name moonshotai/Kimi-K3.1-Pro — no access gate, no form, no waitlist. The download is 96 safetensors shards totalling about 1.56 terabytes.

Kimi K3.1 Pro itself is not brand new. Moonshot announced it on October 23, 2026, as the first model in the K3.1 family. What is new is that the full weights are now public.

The headline figure is 2.8 trillion total parameters, which makes K3.1 Pro the largest publicly downloadable model. It is a mixture-of-experts model: it has 896 experts, and only 16 of them activate for any given token, so 104 billion parameters are active at a time.

The context window is 1 million tokens — 1,048,576, to be exact. In 24 of the model's 93 layers, the usual attention mechanism is replaced by a different design, which is part of how it handles that long context.

"Open weights" and "open source" are not the same thing. The weights ship under Moonshot's own Kimi K3.1-Pro License, not a standard open-source license like Apache 2.0. For independent developers, small teams, and most agencies, that licence is permissive enough to use and build on. Read it before you build a product on it, especially at larger scale.

Moonshot's own numbers put K3.1 Pro near the top of the field. On Terminal-Bench 4.0, a coding-agent benchmark, it performed strongly. As with every vendor benchmark table, treat those numbers as a starting point, not proof — they measure what an agent can do, which is the result that matters most for developers.

The download button is the easy part. The weights are about 1.56 TB, and running them locally needs serious GPU hardware. The hosted price is genuinely competitive: $2 per million input tokens and $10 per million output tokens.

The single most useful thing about a model like K3.1 Pro for a WordPress developer is the combination of long context and strong coding ability. A 1-million-token context means a long-context code audit, or a debugging session across a whole theme or plugin codebase, is realistic in one conversation.

The open weights matter for a second reason specific to WordPress: sites that handle client data can, in principle, run capable models on infrastructure they control, rather than sending everything to a hosted API.

First, do not download 1.56 terabytes on a whim. Try it hosted first — on Kimi.ai, through the API, or through a hosted provider.

Second, if you run a WordPress agency, look at the long-context coding angle before anything else. Try it on a real task: a long-context code audit, or a debugging session across a whole theme.

Third, read the license before you build a product on it. For personal use, small teams, and most agencies, the terms are workable — but check them yourself.

Kimi K3.1 Pro's weights release is the rare AI story this week where the headline and the reality line up — with fine print worth reading.

Grimes to Star in the First-Ever AI-Generated Film Coming to Theaters: Watch a Clip

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The star voices a character in Gods Don't Give Gifts, but the visuals were fully generated by artificial intelligence.

Grimes has been on the cutting edge of the overlap between artificial intelligence and music, and now, she’s doing the same with the technology and film.

As revealed Thursday (Nov. 5), the alternative singer-songwriter is part of the cast of Gods Don’t Give Gifts, the first AI-generated movie coming to theaters. She lends her voice and physical likeness to her character, Zayinth Alda, described in a release as “a disaffected NiiroCore aristocrat and niece to Caen Halvorith, inventor of the Substrate Migration Protocol.”

The film, which will premiere this winter, explores a dystopian future reality in which “consciousness can be transferred between bodies, memory can be bought and sold, and death is no longer final,” a synopsis reads. In a clip released alongside Grimes’ casting announcement, her AI-animated form chastises another character before storming away with a massive, muscular, faceless creature acting as her guard.

“You dragged me to the slag heaps for this? A slideshow of derivative drivel?” she yells before boarding her futuristic spacecraft.

Though the visuals on screen are produced by AI, Grimes’ voice and dialogue were not. Gossip Goblin, the studio behind the Zack London-directed project, asserts in the release, “Casting the likeness of an actor with their spoken vocal performance will become more common with animated films using AI — audiences will see actors, athletes, musicians giving vocal performances with their likeness in films.”

The announcement is fitting, considering Grimes has long been interested in the ways the controversial technology is developing. She’s now gearing up to release her new album, Psy Opera, which explores the complexities of AI and uses artificially generated music on one of the tracks.

This Coffee Subscription Delivers Some of the Freshest Beans We’ve Tried

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It's one of the best gifts you can give to coffee lovers this holiday season.

Our editors and experts handpick every product we feature. We may earn a commission from your purchases.Learn more.

It really doesn't get fresher than this. A Storyville coffee subscription delivers delicious beans right to your doorstep.

We’ve all been there. Standing in the grocery store staring at dozens of coffee brands and trying to decide which one might perk us up, only to end up scowling at a subpar cup of sludge the next morning. Well, those days are over because Storyville, one of Seattle’s best coffee shops, now delivers freshly roasted beans straight to your door.

I tried a Storyville coffee subscription, and I assure you that you’ll never have to waste your time (or money) on stale beans again. Here’s everything you need to know about this caffeinated club.

Storyville is one of the highest-rated coffee shops in Seattle, with locations at Pike Place and at 1st and Madison. They also have a roasting studio you can tour to see exactly where your coffee beans are roasted before making their way to your door. But if you can’t make it to Seattle, they also offer coffee subscription services with nationwide shipping.

The brand sources premium-grade green coffees and roasts each bean varietal individually before blending. Beans are roasted on the darker end of medium and feature a rich, natural sweetness with notes of chocolate and caramel. Unlike many other dark roasts, this coffee has a smooth finish and no bitterness. Best of all, Storyville ships its coffee immediately after roasting.

Storyville subscriptions are highly customizable in order to fit your needs. First, choose between whole beans and single-serve pods. Then choose the quantity and delivery frequency that best suits your needs. You can pause, skip or cancel via text at any time. Moreover, you’ll receive 20% off, among other perks.

I am a coffee fiend. On any given day, I’m three cups in by 9 a.m. That’s why I couldn’t wait to get my hands on a bag of real Seattle coffee.

My order arrived on Wednesday, October 28. I received a bag of Prologue coffee beans, Sunrise espresso beans and Epilogue decaf coffee beans. I knew the roast dates would be stamped on the bags, but I was amazed to see that my beans had been roasted just days prior, on October 25 and October 27. Talk about fresh!

I opened the Prologue first and poured the entire bag into the bean hopper of my De’Longhi Rivelia. After brewing, I took a sip before adding my cream and sugar and was amazed by how smooth it was. It was fresh and flavorful without a hint of bitterness. I love a dark roast and could drink this all day long.

The next day, I opened the Sunrise espresso beans. I used it to make a regular cup of coffee followed by an Americano. Like the Prologue, the beans were clearly fresh, and the coffee was exceptionally smooth! I’ll be saving this bag along with the decaf for espresso martinis with my girlfriends.

As a dark roast gal, I’ve had my fair share of coffee that tastes bitter or burnt. I didn’t get any notes of bitterness from Storyville coffee. I’ll also point out that a bag of Prologue coffee lasted one week. Given how much coffee I drink plus the fact that my husband tends to make a cup here and there, I’m not surprised we went through it so quickly. Luckily, the subscription is completely customizable. If you drink as much coffee as I do, you’ll want to order two or three bags at a time. But if you just enjoy one cup per day, then one bag at a time will suffice.

That said, these beans are so fresh we wouldn’t be surprised if you start drinking more coffee than usual. And we certainly wouldn’t blame you!

It’s also easy to send a subscription as a gift. Simply head over to their gifting page and opt for three, five, eight or 12 prepaid shipments.

GM Patents Launch Control That Watches the Drag Strip Christmas Tree, Launches for You

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The recent patent describes a system that manages the brakes and throttle to deliver remarkably consistent drag strip blasts away from the line.

Getting a great launch at the drag strip takes more than horsepower and sticky tires. Drivers also need to time their start perfectly; even a tenth of a second can make a difference. Now, General Motors has patented technology that could take that last bit of human involvement out of the equation entirely.

The system uses the vehicle's cameras to watch the drag strip's Christmas Tree, predict when the light will turn green, and launch the car automatically. Better yet, it can actually begin preparing the launch before the green light illuminates. We might be about to see the age of push-button drag racing.

The technology is detailed in U.S. Patent 12,780,203, titled “Methods and Systems for Coordinating Launch Control for Vehicles for Racing.” Originally filed in January 2025, the application was published in July 2026 before finally receiving its patent grant on November 3.

At its core, the system builds on the launch control technology already available in modern performance cars. Right now, most launch control systems work very similarly: the driver applies full brakes and full throttle, and holds both pedals until it's time to go; they let off the brake, and the car rockets away. GM's proposed technology takes over most of that process.

The craziest bit is just how it manages all of this. First, it uses on-board cameras to identify the Christmas tree at the drag strip. Once it recognizes the lights, it notifies the driver and prompts them to activate launch control. Those who want to try this new method could control it much the same as they do with existing systems; the patent describes drivers putting their feet on both pedals and then, once the automatic launch system takes over, the driver can remove both feet and let the car work.

The car can maintain braking pressure and staging torque automatically, even after the driver releases the pedals. The clever part comes next: rather than simply waiting for the green light, the software identifies the sequence of yellow lights and compares it against stored timing patterns to calculate precisely when the race will begin.

That's key, because different events run the lights at different times. For example, a Pro Tree illuminates all three amber lights simultaneously before turning green 0.4 seconds later, whereas Sportsman Tree illuminates the amber lights sequentially with half-second intervals. GM's system could account for those differences automatically. The system is even capable of calculating the delay between commanding movement from the vehicle and the vehicle actually moving forward.

That all said, this is just a patent for now. GM doesn't mention any specific vehicle or plan for production in its filing. If it does bring it to market, we'd be eager to see how close it can come to cutting a perfect light.

A home side has scored! It’s Anderlecht, who have gone one up against Salzburg through Ludwig Augustinsson, and it’s an absolute beauty of a free-kick! From a couple of yards outside the penalty area, he curls it over the wall and the keeper doesn’t even move!

Freiburg go one up at Trabzonspor, a tap-in for Lukas Kubler after Matanovic’s header from a corner comes back off the keeper, and so far 100% of tonight’s goals have been scored by away teams.

A team in blue and red stripes has the ball in the net, but there are no Crystal Palace fans celebrating – because it’s Viktoria Plzen going a goal up at Lillestrom.

Play has meanwhile resumed at Thun, but there will be a good four minutes of smoke-related stoppage time to play before half-time.

Hoffenheim’s first significant attack, and it’s a good one! They win the ball back at the edge of their D and break fast, first down the right and then with an early crossfield pass to Alexander Prass, sprinting clear from his own half. He hits a hard, low, first-time shot and Dean Henderson takes the sting off it but not the momentum, and it rolls over the line!

Meanwhile Crystal Palace have the ball in the net against Hoffenheim, but Daichi Kamada was well offside.

Fireworks stop play at Thun! There’s no way anyone can play in the amount of smoke that has settled across the pitch in Switzerland.

What a miss! Jonatan Braut Brunes, the Sparta Prague striker, sends the ball squirming just wide of a gaping Bournemouth goal from an admittedly very firm pull-back.

OK, football is now actually happening in all eight early Europa League kick-offs. Milan v Ferencvaros was the last to get going.

Though actually none of the games I’m monitoring have started yet, with delays including the minute’s silence for Jesus de Miguel, the Tenerife striker who died after an on-pitch collision at the weekend. “Perusing the Sparta Prague team sheet,” writes Peter Oh, “I can’t help but wonder, will the club from the Czech capital be able to stop the Cherries’ run of good form? Mbe Soh. Mbe Soh.”

The players are emerging from their various tunnels, kick-off mere moments away!

Pierre Sage is asked why Axel Disasi is not in the Crystal Palace squad tonight. “It’s a decision I took today.” Is there a reason for that? “No.” Also from Sage:

double quotation markWe are ready to fight and compete in this game. We need to start a new way, because we need points in every competition, and I think the start of this way is tonight. They are a team who like intensity, sprint a lot, and they play forward, it’s very vertical. But they are able to take control of the play inside too. We have to deal with these two things, and we have to take control first of all today.

Bournemouth’s Marco Rose has a chat about momentum, and his eight changes to the lineup today:

double quotation markIt’s always about hard work and we have to maintain momentum every day, in every training, and every game, and today it’s getting a tough challenge. Everyone is in a good spot. The lads who played the last weeks showed up. I hope we have fresh legs. There is no other option for me, because Sunday we have the next game and then again and again.

And on Bournemouth, and the very good place they currently appear to be in:

double quotation markIt’s always about connections and emotions in football. I really feel the commitment of our fans, of the region. Everyone is so happy after hard times in the 90s. To get this kind of experience, it’s just a joy to be part of it and it makes me proud.

Hello world! Another day, another smorgasbord of European delights is being plated up for our delectation tonight. There are, to summarise, too many games going on simultaneously for two eyes to have any hope of following but I’ll do my very best.

Highlights? Well [he writes, copy-and-pasting the preview filed by the Associated Press], Bournemouth travel to Sparta Prague in the Europa League with the aim to stay perfect after four rounds. The English side has been cruising in its maiden European campaign with three wins over Milan, Sturm Graz and Real Sociedad. Bournemouth have also surged to eighth in the Premier League after another three straight wins. “We need to be very reasonable, we know that it’s a long way and I especially know it,” winger Amine Adli, who reached the 2024 Europa League final with Leverkusen, said ahead of the match in Prague. “It’s a long way to the final.”

His former employers also have a 100% record and host pointless Marseille. Milan plays another undefeated club, Ferencvaros, at San Siro, and another Italian giant, Juventus, visit Alkmaar. Struggling Crystal Palace welcome Hoffenheim and Celtic, one of four teams yet to earn a single point, host Besiktas in Glasgow.

In the Conference League, Brighton face Getafe away, while Monaco, up against Danish side Nordsjælland, have been boosted by signing US striker Folarin Balogun.

Zanda Health: Paid Media Coordinator

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Zanda is a fast-growing, globally loved practice management platform that helps health professionals run smooth, efficient practices - and spend more time caring for their patients. We’re used by thousands of clinics across Australia, New Zealand, the UK, the US, and Canada, and we’re just getting started.

Our mission is simple: make health practice management easy and enjoyable.To do that, we need brilliant, passionate people who are excited by innovation, love solving complex problems, and thrive in fast-moving environments.

Are you someone who loves building relationships, spotting new opportunities, and bringing great ideas to life? We're looking for a Paid Media Coordinator to help grow Zanda's reach and connect more health professionals with the tools they need to run successful practices.

In this hands-on role, you'll manage our advertising, sponsorship, and association partnerships across international markets. From discovering new opportunities to promote Zanda and negotiating great deals to coordinating the deliverables and measuring their impact, you'll play an important part in how we grow. You'll work within our Marketing team while having the autonomy to make things happen.

Build and own relationships with associations, publishers, media partners, agencies, and vendors across our key international markets.

Identify and pursue new opportunities for advertising, sponsorships, and partnerships that increase Zanda's brand awareness and reach.

Evaluate and negotiate advertising opportunities, securing competitive rates, valuable placements, and partnerships that deliver results.

Coordinate campaigns from start to finish, working with internal teams and external partners to keep creative assets, approvals, tracking, and deadlines on track.

Manage Programmatic Advertising across channels such as Paid Social and Display, and support opportunities in Paid Search and emerging advertising platforms.

Manage campaign operations, including advertising calendars, budgets, pipelines, and vendor coordination.

Track and evaluate performance, using campaign data and insights to identify what's working, recommend improvements, and maximize the value of our advertising investments.

2-3+ years of relevant experience in marketing, media, partnerships, sponsorships, or campaign management.

Proven ability to build and maintain strong relationships with external partners, vendors, agencies, or stakeholders.

Strong communication and negotiation skills, with the confidence to explore opportunities, discuss terms, and secure mutually beneficial outcomes.

Experience coordinating campaigns or partnerships end-to-end, balancing multiple stakeholders, deliverables, and deadlines.

Excellent organizational skills and attention to detail, with the ability to manage competing priorities independently.

A good understanding of campaign performance and the ability to use data to evaluate results and make practical recommendations, alongside experience supporting creative briefs, advertising copy, or landing-page improvements.

Experience working with professional associations, publishers, sponsorships, or media partnerships across international markets.

Familiarity with Paid Social, Display, Paid Search, or emerging advertising channels.

Experience with tools such as HubSpot, Google Analytics, Canva, Adobe Creative Suite, or project management platforms.

An understanding of marketing metrics such as CAC, CPA, ROAS, attribution, or UTM tracking.

Experience in SaaS, HealthTech, or using AI and automation tools to improve marketing workflows.

A natural relationship builder. You enjoy connecting with people, creating genuine partnerships, and aren't afraid to negotiate or ask for a better deal.

Proactive and resourceful. You spot opportunities, take initiative, and find ways to move things forward without waiting to be asked.

Organized and detail-oriented. You can juggle multiple moving pieces while keeping track of the small details that make a big difference.

Someone who takes ownership. You're comfortable making decisions, following through on commitments, and being accountable for outcomes.

Adaptable and collaborative. You enjoy working with different people, navigating changing priorities and company direction, and finding solutions along the way.

Juventus, Massara: "Le assenze sono numerose, ma non vogliamo alibi. Abbiamo risorse all'altezza"

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Frederic Massara, DS della Juventus, è intervenuto ai microfoni di SkySport nel prepartita della sfida di Europa League contro l'Az Alkmaar. Ecco le sue dichiarazioni:

"Il mister sta gestendo una situazione complessa, le assenze sono numerose, ma abbiamo delle risorse all'altezza e ci aspettiamo che lo dimostrino. Non vogliamo alibi. Penso che il messaggio di Spalletti vada letto come un richiamo alle responsabilità di tutti, non dobbiamo aspettare che il momento passi o che cambi qualcosa intorno a noi. Abbiamo ribadito totale fiducia al mister e vogliamo dimostrarlo. Alajbegovic? È un talento che era seguito da mezza Europa e anche dalla Juventus prima del mio arrivo. C'è un team che si confronta e cerca di fare investimenti. Ancora è molto giovane e dobbiamo dargli il tempo di adattarsi ad un campionato così difficile come quello italiano. L'italianità è un elemento essenziale nel campionato, non è solamente stata la forza della Juve, ma anche di altri club. Ritrovare un nucleo italiano forte è una necessità, bilanciato con calciatori stranieri che ci permettano di creare un blocco compatto".

Everything Announced at GOG’s First-Ever Showcase Event

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Covering the video games industry since 2017, with experience in news, articles, and reviews. Currently a writer at DualShockers and gaming magazines.

I know that I'm not the only one who has always appreciated GOG as a sacred sanctuary for classic PC titles, but its latest event proves the platform is doing much more than just keeping old games running.

That's because, to celebrate its 18th anniversary, the team held its first-ever showcase, and it was packed with updates that have me genuinely excited about where the platform is heading – and you should be too!

Broadcast today, Thursday, November 5, on YouTube, the first-ever GOG Showcase caught everyone's attention with a variety of announcements, ranging from new games and features to an entirely new publishing initiative.

For starters, co-founder Miho Kinski perfectly summed up the platform's philosophy at the beginning of the event, making it clear that its mission remains unchanged: "We wanted games to live forever. Not for a season, not until the servers shut down, but for as long as people want them to play." And honestly, that message really resonated throughout the presentation.

Then, the event kicked off by celebrating the GOG Patrons program, and the numbers are pretty impressive: more than 10,000 supporters have helped preserve over 300 games, ensuring they run flawlessly on modern systems with the help of more than 1,700 technical fixes.

Bit Kid shadowdrops Knytt Classic for free on Steam and GOG.

And speaking of games, the showcase also had plenty of nostalgia in store, with the announcement of Wing Commander Anniversary Editions. The series is returning in grand fashion with its 35th and 30th Anniversary collections, featuring both legacy and updated versions of the games. Oh, and if you already own these titles, the new editions will automatically appear in your library!

The expansion of GOG's classic game collection doesn't stop there, either. The original Road Rash has also officially joined the platform today, giving fans another opportunity to revisit this beloved classic.

The event kicked off by celebrating the GOG Patrons program, and the numbers are pretty impressive: more than 10,000 supporters have helped preserve over 300 games, ensuring they run flawlessly on modern systems with the help of more than 1,700 technical fixes.

However, perhaps the biggest surprise was the reveal of Retrovibe by GOG, a new publishing initiative focused on bringing previously unreleased games with a "classic soul" to modern audiences. This marks the company's first major step into publishing, and it's an interesting evolution for a store that has spent nearly two decades acting as a digital harbor for gaming history.

Fans can look forward to bringing lost games back from the dead.

And when it comes to new features, that wasn't all the showcase had to offer. GOG also introduced GOG Social, a new social network integrated into the platform that allows users to share screenshots, videos, and participate in discussions, all directly connected to the games they own.

Finally, the team confirmed what many of us have been asking for: a completely rebuilt GOG GALAXY. The launcher has been redesigned for Windows and Mac, promising better performance and, most importantly, full Linux support. Yeah... I used to pray for times like this!

Eighteen years of keeping games alive is no small feat, and based on these announcements, the next chapter for GOG looks just as bright. As the team emphasized during its first-ever showcase, "18 years of making games live forever together." I've always relied on the platform to revisit some of my favorite childhood titles, but with these new social features and its expansion into publishing, I have a feeling I'll be spending a lot more time exploring its library in the years to come!

The Video Game History Foundation just tripled its public research library!
